Text hash c353e28c8e736ed1b8c4f814d02fcf08399d6b00dd8137ea73726847016761ba; absolute characters 0–1795; files: jobs/hathi-gpo-orders/output/texts/EO_6899.txt

```text
# Copy: machine read government printed copy; not authenticated as an original.
# Source: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Executive_Orders_6690-6935.djvu
# Location: Executive_Orders_6690-6935.djvu; scanned pages 535-537; closing number 6899
----- EXACT SOURCE TEXT -----
DEFOE ce vi ee -

UNITED STATES OF AMER!L-


|

Executibe @rder

EXEMPTION OF THomas M. TuHurRSsTON FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FoR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,
That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires: * * *”;

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Thomas M. Thurston,
deputy collector in charge of the Liquidating Division, Office of the Collector of
Customs, Treasury Department, New York, New York, who, during the current
month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the
service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service
until December 1, 1935;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section, I do hereby exempt Thomas M. Thurston from the provisions thereof and
continue him in the service until December 1, 1935.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
THe Waite Hovss,
November 8, 1934.

[No. 6899]

```

```text
Executive Grder

EXEMPTION OF WILLIAM GERIG FRoM CoMPpuULSORY RETIREMENT FOR
AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C.,
sec. 715a), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Governinent or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,
That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *”;

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that William Gerig, head engineer,
Engineer Department at Large, War Department, who was exempted from compulsory
retirement for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 7315 of March 11, 1936,
be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service
until April 1, 1938;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt William Gerig from the provisions

thereof and continue him in the service until April 1, 1938.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tae Wuite House,
March 24, 1937.

[No. 7586]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1937

```

### T0832180e53169748d79ff695

Text hash ded6eb8b9df197dddc41faa2768b6712b089c4386d1d77a63e3f75eac4746aa0; absolute characters 172–1073; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-app-executive-orders-6756A-a8fb8f3aff.txt

```text
Roosevelt, President of the United States, do hereby offer to enter into an agreement with the members of such service trades not heretofore codified as shall hereafter be designated by the Administrator for Industrial Recovery, whereunder any such member displaying appropriate NRA insignia shall evidence his agreement to comply with the standards of labor approved by the Administrator, on the condition, however, that in any locality in which eighty-five percent (85%) of the members of any such designated trade shall propose to agree with me to abide by any local code of fair trade practices suggested by them for that locality, after approval of such local code by the Administrator, no member of such trade in such locality shall be entitled to display such NRA insignia, unless, in addition to the provisions of the said standards of labor, he is complying with all terms of such local code.
```

```text
By: W. A. Harriman
Administrative Officer

Tue White House
Jan. 22, 1936

[No. 6949-A]

U.S GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938

§ |
Ae

Executive Order

EXTENSION OF PERIOD OF ELIGIBILITY FOR APPOINTMENT AS OFFICERS IN
THE FOREIGN SERVICE

Notwithstanding the provisions of article I, paragraph 3, section (h) of Consular
Regulations, 1896, as amended by Executive Order No. 5642, of June 8, 1931, the
period of eligibility of those candidates for appointment as Foreign Service officers
whose names were on the eligible list on March 20, 1934, is hereby extended one year
and a half from the date on which their eligibility for appointment would otherwise
expire.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

Tue WuirTe House,

January 23, 1935.

[No. 6950]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938
```

```text
# Copy: machine read government printed copy; not authenticated as an original.

Va oY THE

‘Sid LU STATS OF AMERICA

OCT 2 ‘35


——T

J
3/
AG

Executibe Order

EXEMPTION OF JOSEPH L. SMITH FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT FOR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for
automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued
in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions
of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires
* * *),

?

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Joseph L. Smith, a mech-
anician in the Senate Office Building under the Office of the Architect of the Capitol,
who was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 2 years by Executive
Order No. 6263, of August 30, 1933, be further exempted from the provisions of this
section and continued in the service until August 31, 1936:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section, I do hereby exempt Joseph L. Smith from the provisions thereof and con-
tinue him in the service until August 31, 1936.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Waite House,
August 29, 1985.

[No. 7167]

```

```text
Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF ALBERT §. HITCHCOCK FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FoR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:

of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires

* * * ” ;

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Albert S. Hitchcock, prin-
cipal botanist, Bureau of Plant Industry, Department of Agriculture, who, during
the cucrent month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation
from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in
the service until October 1, 1936:

by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Albert S. Hitchcock from the provisions
thereof and continue him in the service until October 1, 1936.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
```

### T537d107ac57a324d86a677ea

Text hash d6eb9349dbdfbdeefb2773254dc6a0a8fecfe3729b1cabe9b241e976c9601783; absolute characters 0–1840; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6969.txt

```text
DEPOSITED FY THE
YRITED STATUS CFP MERICA

Wie | 3 35

\

Nosy CT

t\

Executive Order

Cope or Farr CoMPETITION FOR THE CIGARETTE, SNUFF, CHEWING,
AND SMOKING Tospacco MANUFACTURING INDUSTRY

An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with
the provisions of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16,
1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Cigarette, Snuff, Chew-
ing, and Smoking Tobacco Manufacturing Industry; and hearings having been duly
held thereon; and the annexed report on said Code, containing findings with respect
thereto, having been made and directed to me:

NOW, THEREFORE, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States,
pursuant to the authority vested in me by said Title of said Act, and otherwise, do
hereby adopt and approve said report and findings; incorporate the same herein by
reference; find further that the approval of said Code will be in the public interest;
and order that said Code of Fair Competition be and it hereby is approved.

I further order that the Division of Research and Planning of the National Re-
covery Administration be and it hereby is directed to make a study of conditions in
the Industry relating to wages and hours of labor of employees to determine the extent
of the contribution made by the Industry toward reemployment and increased pur-
chasing power, and submit its report thereon together with its recommendations with
respect thereto to the National Industrial Recovery Board as soon as practicable in
order that said Board may, after due notice and hearing, take such action in connection
therewith as it may deem necessary and proper to effectuate the purposes of said
Title of said Act.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tur Wuite House
Feb. 9, 1986.

[No. 6969]

US GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938

```

```text
Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF T. WaRREN ALLEN FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FoR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C.,
sec. 715a), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions
of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires

sz * wre

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that T. Warren Allen, principal
highway engineer, Bureau of Public Roads, Department of Agriculture, who was
exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of one year by Executive Order
No. 6919, of December 12, 1934, be further exempted from the provisions of this
section and continued in the service until January 1, 1937:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt T. Warren Allen from the provisions
thereof and continue him in the service until January 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
THe Wuite House,
December 16, 1936.

[No. 7250]

```

```text
Executive @rder

EXEMPTION OF WILLIAM E. CoTTron FROM CoMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FoR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C.,
title 5, sec. 715a), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provi-
sions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so
requires * * *”

veterinarian, Bureau of Animal Industry, Department of Agriculture, who, during
the month of September, 1936, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and
continued in the service until October 1, 1937:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt William E. Cotton from the provisions
thereof and continue him in the service until October 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

Tue Wuire Hovss,

August 11, 1936.
```

```text
# Copy: machine read government printed copy; not authenticated as an original.

Sst OR TORY THE
Yih Cia.ds OF AMERICs


|
Ae

Executibe Order

EXEMPTION OF Henry C. STUART FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT FOR
AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:

Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions
of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires:

* * *") ;

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Henry C. Stuart, Assistant
Collector of Customs, Bureau of Customs, Treasury Department, New York, New
York, who, in February 1935, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and
continued in the service until March 1, 1936;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section, I do hereby exempt Henry C. Stuart from the provisions thereof and continue
him in the service until March 1, 1936.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

Tue Waite Hovssg,

December 28, 1984.

[No. 6932]

```

```text
WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (6 U.S.C.,
sec. 715a), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,
That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *”;

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Miss Jessie McI.. Watson,
reviser, Catalogue Division, Library of Congress, who was exempted from compulsory
retirement for a period of one year by Exccutive Order No. 7094 of July 8, 1935, be
further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service
until August 1, 1937:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Miss Jessie Mcl. Watson from the

provisions thereof and continue her in the service until August 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Waite Hovss,
June 19, 1936.
```

```text
|
AG

Executive @rder

EXEMPTION OF WILLIAM McNEIR From ComMPuLsory RETIREMENT FOR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S. C.,
sec. 715a), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,
That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *”;

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that William McNeir, Chief of
the Bureau of Accounts, Department of State, who was exempted from compulsory
retirement for a period of two years by Executive Orders No. 6865 of October 4,
1934, and No. 7205 of October 14, 1935, be further exempted from the provisions of this

section and continued in the service until May 1, 1937;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt William McNeir from the provisions

thereof and continue him in the service until May 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Wuite Houses,
October 24, 1936.

[No. 7477]

1) S GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1936
```

```text
WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C.,
sec. 715a), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the pro-
visions of this section any person when, in bis judgment, the public interest so
requires * * *77;

will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service,
be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until
```

```text
WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404
(5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,

section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *”;
```

```text
DEPOSITED BY TH:
ONITED STATES OF AKEK.C-

NOV 29 ‘2%

5 |
Ac

Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF CARLETON W. STURTEVANT FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FoR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404
(5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions
of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires:

* s *”,

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Carleton W. Sturtevant,
principal engineer, Engineer Department at Large, War Department, who was
exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of twenty-one months by Execu-
tive Orders No. 6577, of January 25, 1934, and No. 6773, of June 30, 1934, be further
exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until
May 1, 1936;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in
me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Carleton W. Sturtevant from the
provisions thereof and continue him in the service until May 1, 1936.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

THe Wuite Hovsz,

October 30, 1936.

[No. 7217]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1935

```

```text
WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404
(5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions
of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires:

* * *”.

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Curtis F. Marbut, principal
soil scientist, Division of Soil Survey, Bureau of Chemistry and Soils, Department
of Agriculture, who was 70 years of age on July 19, 1933, and who was exempted
until July 1, 1935, from compulsory retirement by Executive Orders Nos. 6214 and
6738, dated July 25, 1933, and June 14, 1934, respectively, be further exempted from
the provisions of this section and continued in the service until July 1, 1936;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section, I do hereby exempt Curtis F. Marbut from the provisions thereof and con-
tinue him in the service until July 1, 1936.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
```

```text
Executive Order
EXEMPTION OF POST WHEELER FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FOR AGE
WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch . 314 , 47 Stat . 382 , 404 ;
5 U.S.C. , sec . 692b ) , provides :
" On and after July 1 , 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service , applicable to such person , shall be continued in such
service , notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary : Provided ,
That the President may, by Executive order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when , in his judgment, the public interest so requires
* * * ."
AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Post Wheeler , Envoy Extra￾ordinary and Minister Plenipotentiary to Albania , who, during the month of August
1934 , will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the
service , be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service
for such time as may be deemed necessary , not in excess of 2 months from August 6 ,
1934 :
NOW, THEREFORE , by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section , I do hereby exempt Post Wheeler from the provisions thereof and continue
him in the service for such time as may be deemed necessary , not in excess of 2 months
from August 6, 1934 .
```

### Tb8ee53e9e929de693fa8e4e2

Text hash 964601b7691078b8b31b539a6185f4082725bbe3ebab66f013c2857f0d896102; absolute characters 0–1449; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6756A.txt

```text
DEPOSITEO BY THE
UNITED STATES OF AMERICA

JUL 30 %

Executive Order

LocaL CopEs FoR UNCODIFIED SERVICE TRADES

By virtue of authority vested in me under Title I of the National Industrial
Recovery Act, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, do hereby
offer to enter into an agreement with the members of such service trades not hereto-
fore codified as shall hereafter be designated by the Administrator for Industrial
Recovery, whereunder any such member displaying appropriate NRA insignia shall
evidence his agreement to comply with the standards of labor approved by the Ad-
ministrator, on the condition, however, that in any locality in which eighty-five
percent (85%) of the members of any such designated trade shall propose to agree
with me to abide by any local code of fair trade practices suggested by them for that
locality, after approval of such local code by the Administrator, no member of such
trade in such locality shall be entitled to display such NRA insignia, unless, in addi-
tion to the provisions of the said standards of labor, he is complying with all terms
of such local code.

The Administrator may supplement this Order by such rules, regulations, ex-
ceptions, modifications, conditions and determinations as, in his opinion, shall

effectuate the purposes of this Order and of said Act.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
THe Waite Hovss,
June 28, 1934.

[No. 6756-A]

U, S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1996

```

```text
EXEMPTION oF LysteR H. Dewey From CompuLsory RETIREMENT

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,
That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires: * * *”;

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Lyster H. Dewey, senior
botanist, Bureau of Plant Industry, Department of Agriculture, who, during the
current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation
from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the
service until October 1, 1935;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section, I do hereby exempt Lyster H. Dewey from the provisions thereof and con-

tinue him in the service until October 1, 1935.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
THe Waite Houvuss,
March 8, 1935.
```

```text
Executive Order
EXTENSION OF PERIOD OF ELIGIBILITY FOR APPOINTMENT AS OFFICERS IN
THE FOREIGN SERVICE
Notwithstanding the provisions of article I, paragraph 3 , section (h) of Consular
Regulations , 1896 , as amended by Executive Order No. 5642 , of June 8 , 1931 , the
period of eligibility of those candidates for appointment as Foreign Service officers
whose names were on the eligible list on March 20, 1934, is hereby extended one year
and a half from the date on which their eligibility for appointment would otherwise
expire .
```

```text
DEPOSITED BY THE
UNITED STATES OF AMERICA

APR 9 °86

KURE ISLAND
{OCEAN ISLAND)


ey CY

a

Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF HENRY C. StuarT FROM ComPuLsory RETIREMENT
FOR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (56 U.S.C.,
sec. 715a), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,
That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *”;

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Henry C. Stuart, assistant
collector of customs, Bureau of Customs, Treasury Department, New York, New
York, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 1 year by Execu-
tive Order No. 6932, of December 28, 1934, be further exempted from the provisions
of this section and continued in the service until March 1, 1937:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Henry C. Stuart from the provisions
thereof and continue him in the service until March 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
```

```text
EXEMPTION OF HucH A. Morrison FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FoR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
U.S. C., title 5, sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the Dis-
trict of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions
of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires:

* * *”.

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Hugh A. Morrison, custodian
of the House Reading Room, Library of Congress, who was exempted from compul-
sory retirement for a period of 2 years by Executive Order No. 6106, dated April 10,
1933, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the
service until May 1, 1937;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in
me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Hugh A. Morrison from the pro-

visions thereof and continue him in the service until May 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Wuite House,
April 10, 1985.
```

```text
FurRTHER EXEMPTION OF THoMAs M. THURSTON FROM COMPULSORY

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C.,
sec. 715a), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions
of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires

in the service until December 1, 1936:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested
in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby further exempt Thomas M. Thurston
from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until December 1, 1936.
```

### Te554d357ada5b9f17ef88c2a

Text hash ebf941edb52472728b05c99cd54ee868583762bddd1d358b040013798bd82c75; absolute characters 0–1693; files: jobs/hathi-gpo-orders/output/texts/EO_7120A.txt

```text
# Copy: machine read government printed copy; not authenticated as an original.
# Source: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Executive_Orders_7082-7261.djvu
# Location: Executive_Orders_7082-7261.djvu; scanned pages 105-105; closing number 7120A

Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF WILLIAM H. NELSON FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FoR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404
(5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions
of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires:

» 6 +.

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that William H. Nelson, park
ranger, Yosemite National Park, National Park Service, Department of the Interior,
who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and
continued in the service until August 1, 1936:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section, I do hereby exempt William H. Nelson from the provisions thereof and
continue him in the service until August 1, 1936.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tus Waits Hovsz,
July%30519365,

[No. 7120-A]

```

### Te7f806d7307c7a3b4e348abc

Text hash f160df628076e76e52ba53e2b77af5bcd939d612d6e95083d98bfc5e8985c9bb; absolute characters 106–1007; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6756-local-codes-for-uncodified-service-trades.txt

```text
Roosevelt, President of the United States, do hereby offer to enter into an agreement with the members of such service trades not heretofore codified as shall hereafter be designated by the Administrator for Industrial Recovery, whereunder any such member displaying appropriate NRA insignia shall evidence his agreement to comply with the standards of labor approved by the Administrator, on the condition, however, that in any locality in which eighty-five percent (85%) of the members of any such designated trade shall propose to agree with me to abide by any local code of fair trade practices suggested by them for that locality, after approval of such local code by the Administrator, no member of such trade in such locality shall be entitled to display such NRA insignia, unless, in addition to the provisions of the said standards of labor, he is complying with all terms of such local code.
```

### Tf0a69ff39fbab1a70ff626aa

Text hash 55135c5ed52d52b1d8f012ca3e7aa662b18ae903c64bc6b16c80948bf8fb037a; absolute characters 0–1794; files: jobs/hathi-gpo-orders/output/texts/EO_6834.txt

```text
# Copy: machine read government printed copy; not authenticated as an original.
# Source: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Executive_Orders_6690-6935.djvu
# Location: Executive_Orders_6690-6935.djvu; scanned pages 369-369; closing number 6834
----- EXACT SOURCE TEXT -----
Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF Post WHEELER FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FoR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
5 U.S.C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,
That the President may, by Executive order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * * .”

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Post Wheeler, Envoy Extra-
ordinary and Minister Plenipotentiary to Albania, who, during the month of August
1934, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the
service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service
for such time as may be deemed necessary, not in excess of 2 months from August 6,
1934:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section, I do hereby exempt Post Wheeler from the provisions thereof and continue
him in the service for such time as may be deemed necessary, not in excess of 2 months
from August 6, 1934.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.

THe Wuire House,

August 29, 1934.

[No. 6834]

```


(1935-10-30) is a single-clause order under section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 exempting Carleton W. Sturtevant from compulsory retirement for age and continuing the person in the service 'until May 1, 1936'. The order ends by its own terms on 1936-05-01 (stated date, inclusive; exclusive alternative one day earlier, per the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling on 'until' dates). Later orders in the same person's exemption chain, read in the packet: none held; each is recorded as a later event with its own date and quote (owner ruling, 2026-10-21), and none revokes, amends or shortens this order. Last held order in the Sturtevant chain (EO 6577 → 6773 → 7217). jobs/namier finds no later held text naming Carleton W. Sturtevant; the OFR catalog starts after this order's end. Whether he separated on or before May 1, 1936 is not in any held source and is not asserted. The dossier's machine term leads were read against the text; the operative until-date in the command sentence is the end. @rationale:retirement:statute @rationale:retirement:search @rationale:retirement:inventory\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms expiry on the stated date, recorded per the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling as the stated date inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the alternative exclusive reading (the exemption ends when that day begins) is one day earlier and is stated here for the human reviewer. The person's death, resignation or separation before the stated date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an earlier termination of the order; no held record shows any such event. A later 'further exemption' of the same person signed before this order's stated date is a new, overlapping grant that neither revokes nor shortens this order's term (it recites this order and adds a later end date), so it is recorded as a later event, not as an earlier ending. No later order amends, revokes or extends this order before its stated date (see the search). The until-date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline or not-later-than bound on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command: the time-limited exemption of the named person (or persons) from section 204 and their continuance in the service until the stated date. The recital of the statute, the quotation of its proviso and the identification of the employee, age and position are not independent commands. No retained independent command survives the stated term.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and compensation earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects of the person's continued employment; the order creates no continuing duty, office or rule after its stated term. this one.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole order is one clause covering one named person with one fixed term; no partial amendment or revocation of any part is held or found; the later 'further exemption' grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (an archived capture whose archive and WARC location are recorded) has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the clause-drafts copy. EO 7315 (March 11, 1936), read in full, exempts William Gerig, head engineer, Engineer Department at Large, War Department, from section 204 of the Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until April 1, 1937. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page record, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Later orders naming the same employee are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 7586 (March 24, 1937) recites that Gerig 'was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 7315 of March 11, 1936' and further exempts him until April 1, 1938. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. The alternative reading, that the later orders prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at 1938-04-01 (inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no later order operates on this order's text. Legal reading: a time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1937-04-01, inclusive (exclusive alternative: it stopped when that day began). The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming this employee beyond those listed. Searches made on 2026-10-28: census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv, whitespace-insensitive) for the employee's surname among compulsory-retirement orders, and a full-text search of every held order text under jobs/*/output/texts for the employee's name. The machine USC and eCFR citation searches (complete, 0 hits) exclude a current-law reference; the OFR catalog search starts in 1937 and is not relied on alone. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7704 of September 10, 1937, running to October 1, 1938, and EO 7725 of October 12, 1937, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act. No held record shows the employee's death or separation before that date; if such a record is found, the claim must be reopened to decide whether the exemption lapsed earlier. No held order amends, revokes or shortens this order. Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. The alternative reading, that the later orders prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last later order's 'until' date (1938-04-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no later order operates on this order's text.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date. Later orders (EO 7586) recite it as a past grant and grant new exemptions; they do not keep this order operative.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; the later further-exemption orders are recorded as later events and do not amend this order.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"598b801998b90c1a6da1a9c693191b2e33b12e1c069394fc9dc601a6642eca67","path":"research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1a/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1a/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:33","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"598b801998b90c1a6da1a9c693191b2e33b12e1c069394fc9dc601a6642eca67","path":"research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1a/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:33","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:7315#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"fcff731de9a8f5707d3785ce020f82c64695e2f1bcc48aa3a14091b3c16ea456","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7315-exemption-william-gerig-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"fcff731de9a8f5707d3785ce020f82c64695e2f1bcc48aa3a14091b3c16ea456","state":"match"},"sha256":"3ed01a6666c739ad37e410ba5c00c411573f11db1ddf42719cc6aa986d526fe4","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7315-exemption-william-gerig-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"fcff731de9a8f5707d3785ce020f82c64695e2f1bcc48aa3a14091b3c16ea456"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"aa2a6036732d3ba842877718d4baaac9cb223991218bda1683cdfc889e1e3f4a","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7586-exemption-william-gerig-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"aa2a6036732d3ba842877718d4baaac9cb223991218bda1683cdfc889e1e3f4a","state":"match"},"sha256":"e84109d71eab7b62bf94529a30374f7bff849859af73a7571e067a768d3e7250","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/te
